# Writing Team Professional Reference Corpus Index

**Version:** 1.1.0  
**Status:** SUPPORT / REFERENCE INDEX  
**Supersedes:** v1.0.0  
**Purpose:** Route the Writing Team to the smallest relevant set of professional reference transcripts without scanning the full corpora.

## 1. Operating Rule

This artifact is a **locator, not a professional authority and not a synthesized writing guide**. The underlying corpora remain separate, intact, attributable sources.

Use this sequence:

1. Identify the actual writing, editing, publishing, reader-experience, bookselling, or production question.
2. Search this index by topic, technique, platform, persona-relevant term, or title language.
3. Select the smallest useful set of candidate references.
4. Retrieve those exact transcripts from the named source corpus using the locator.
5. Read the source transcript before applying its advice.
6. Preserve source identity and disagreement. Do not average, merge, harmonize, or manufacture consensus between professional sources.
7. Apply any technique only when it serves the user's intent, canon, manuscript evidence, project stage, and Voice & Style.

**Do not use this index as a substitute for reading a selected transcript.** Metadata tells the team where to look; the transcript supplies the actual professional content.

### Staleness rule

If any corpus SHA-256 above changes, treat this index as stale for that corpus and regenerate the index from the revised source. A changed corpus does not authorize changes to any other corpus.

## 3. Writing-Team Retrieval Guide

These are **search-entry points**, not source rankings.

| Writing-Team use | Start with index terms such as |
|---|---|
| Writer / Novelist | Character; Plot & Structure; Story Structure; Scene Craft; Dialogue; Point of View; Worldbuilding; Theme; Description; Sentence Craft; Genre; Conflict & Stakes |
| Editor | Editing & Revision; Revision & Editing; Copyediting; Proofreading; Feedback; Beta Readers; Developmental Editing; Sentence Craft; Pacing; Scene Craft; Continuity |
| Publisher | Publishing; Literary Agents; Query Letters; Market Positioning; Self-Publishing; Amazon KDP; IngramSpark; Book Distribution & Publishing Platforms; Book Marketing & Sales; Pricing; Book Covers; Bookstore Stocking; Distribution; Book Launch |
| Reader | Reader Experience & Engagement; Reader Engagement; Bookselling & Reader Matching; Reader Trends & Genre; Hooks; Audience; Genre Conventions; First Chapter; Pacing; Character; Climax |
| Screenwriter / adaptation analysis | Scene Craft; Story Structure; Dialogue; Character; Conflict & Stakes; Pacing; Story Analysis & Examples |

## 7. Maintenance Notes

- Regenerate this artifact when a source corpus changes materially.
- Do not copy transcript prose into this index.
- Do not create cross-source consensus statements in this index.
- New corpora must remain independent source identities with their own code, hash, locator scheme, and topic metadata.
- If a transcript lacks adequate metadata, enrich its metadata or retrieval title without rewriting the transcript body.
- Source-specific expertise remains attributable to that source; this index does not convert source perspective into Writing Team policy.
